## Summary of Keyboard/Key Event Handling Code in the Codebase

Based on my comprehensive search through the codebase, here are all the files that contain keyboard/key event handling code:

### **Primary Keyboard Handling Files:**

1. **`src/runtime/input.rs`** (Main keyboard handler)
   - **Function**: `handle_key()` (lines 10-413)
     - Primary entry point for all keyboard input processing
     - Handles keyboard shortcuts, keybindings, and text input
     - Returns `Option<Cmd>`
   - **Numpad Shortcuts** (lines 24-51): Numpad1-4, NumpadAdd, NumpadSubtract
   - **Modal Key Handler** (lines 415-477): `handle_modal_key()` function
   - **Key Processing Includes**:
     - Multi-cursor shortcuts (Option+Option+Arrow) with double-tap detection
     - Undo/Redo (Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Cmd+Shift+Z, Cmd+Y)
     - Text editing (Save, Select All, Copy, Cut, Paste, Duplicate)
     - Navigation (arrows, Home/End, Page Up/Down, word navigation)
     - Tab/split management
     - Modal navigation
     - Selection management

2. **`src/runtime/app.rs`** (Event loop integration)
   - **Modifier State Tracking** (lines 31, 39, 64, 171-173)
   - **KeyboardInput Event Handler** (lines 175-237)
     - Detects Alt/Option key press states
     - Double-tap detection for Option key (lines 182-192)
     - Modifier key extraction (lines 220-223):
       - `control_key()`
       - `shift_key()`
       - `alt_key()`
       - `super_key()` (macOS Command key)
     - Debug shortcuts (F2, F7, F8) - debug assertions only
     - Calls `handle_key()` with all parameters
   - **Option Double-Tap Feature** (lines 44-45, 69-70, 184-191)

3. **`src/main.rs`** (Test integration)
   - **Lines 39-597**: Keyboard handling tests
   - **Tests** (lines 44-595):
     - Arrow key selection tests
     - Navigation tests
     - Multi-cursor tests
   - **Test Helper**: `test_model_with_selection()` (lines 57-102)

### **Supporting Files:**

4. **`src/messages.rs`** (Message types)
   - **EditorMsg** enum (lines 18-110): Editor keyboard actions
   - **DocumentMsg** enum (lines 114-143): Text editing from keyboard
   - **ModalMsg** enum (lines 149-176): Modal keyboard navigation
   - **LayoutMsg** enum (lines 202-255): Tab/split keyboard shortcuts
   - **AppMsg** enum (lines 259-277): Application-level keyboard actions

5. **`src/commands.rs`** (Command palette keybindings)
   - **CommandDef** struct with keybinding field (lines 49-53)
   - **COMMANDS** registry (lines 56-147): Keybinding definitions for all commands
   - Static display of keybindings (e.g., "⌘S", "⇧⌘Z", "⌘L")

### **Key Code Locations:**

| Feature | File | Location |
|---------|------|----------|
| **Main keyboard dispatch** | `src/runtime/input.rs` | Lines 10-413 |
| **Modal keyboard handling** | `src/runtime/input.rs` | Lines 415-477 |
| **KeyboardInput event handling** | `src/runtime/app.rs` | Lines 175-237 |
| **Modifier state tracking** | `src/runtime/app.rs` | Lines 31, 39, 171-223 |
| **Option double-tap detection** | `src/runtime/app.rs` | Lines 181-192, 232 |
| **Numpad shortcuts** | `src/runtime/input.rs` | Lines 24-51 |
| **Debug shortcuts (F2, F7, F8)** | `src/runtime/app.rs` | Lines 196-218 |
| **Message definitions** | `src/messages.rs` | Lines 18-292 |
| **Keybinding registry** | `src/commands.rs` | Lines 49-147 |

### **Key Event Types Handled:**

- **Arrow Keys**: Up, Down, Left, Right (with/without Shift, Option, Cmd)
- **Named Keys**: Home, End, PageUp, PageDown, Tab, Enter, Escape, Space, Backspace, Delete
- **Function Keys**: F2, F7, F8 (debug only)
- **Character Keys**: a-z for shortcuts (e.g., Cmd+S, Cmd+Z)
- **Numpad Keys**: Numpad1-4, NumpadAdd, NumpadSubtract
- **Modifier Detection**: Control, Shift, Alt/Option, Logo/Super (Cmd)

### **Special Features:**

- Option double-tap detection (300ms window) for multi-cursor shortcuts
- Physical key support for numpad detection
- Cross-platform modifier handling (Ctrl on Windows/Linux, Cmd on macOS)
- Modal focus capture (keyboard input routed to modal when active)
- Character input filtering when modifiers are held

src/runtime/input.rs, src/runtime/app.rs, src/main.rs, src/messages.rs, src/commands.rs

model.editor_mut().clear_selection();\n372:                 update(model, Msg::Editor(EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::Down)))\n373:             } else {\n374:                 update(model, Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::DeleteWordBackward))\n393:         }\n394:         Key::Named(NamedKey::Backspace) => {\n395:             update(model, Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::DeleteBackward))\n396: update(model, Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::InsertChar(' ')))\n400:         }\n401: \n402:         // Character input (only when no Ctrl/Cmd)\n403:         Key::Character(ref s) if !(ctrl || logo) => {\n404:             let mut cmd = None;\n405:             for ch in s.chars() {\n406:                 cmd = update(model, Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::InsertChar(ch))).or(cmd);\n407:             }\n408: keys to the modal instead of the editor.\n418: #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]\n419: fn handle_modal_key(\n420:     model: &mut AppModel,\n421:     key: Key,\n422:     ctrl: bool,\n423:     _shift: bool,\n424:     alt: bool,\n425:     logo: bool,\n426: ) -> Option<Cmd> {\n427:     match key {\n428:         // Escape: close modal\n429:         Key::Named(NamedKey::Escape) => update(model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::Close))),\n430: \n431:         // Enter: confirm modal action\n432:         Key::Named(NamedKey::Enter) => update(model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::Confirm))),\n433: \n434:         // Arrow keys for navigation in modal lists\n435:         Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowUp) => {\n436:             update(model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::SelectPrevious)))\n437: + Arrow)\n443:         Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowLeft) if alt => {\n444:             update(model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::MoveCursorWordLeft)))\n445:         }\n446:         Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowRight) if alt => {\n447:             update(model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::MoveCursorWordRight)))\n448:         }\n449: \n450:         // Word deletion (Option/Alt + Backspace)\n451:         Key::Named(NamedKey::Backspace) if alt => {\n452:             update(model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::DeleteWordBackward)))\n453:         }\n454: \n455:         // Commands represent side effects that should be performed after an update.\n4: \n5: use std::path::PathBuf;\n6: \n7: // ============================================================================\n8: // Command Palette Registry\n9: // ============================================================================\n10: \n11: /// Identifies a command that can be executed via the command palette\n12: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\n13: pub enum CommandId {\n14:     // File operations\n15:     NewFile,\n16:     SaveFile,\n17: \n18:     // Edit operations\n19:     Undo,\n20:     Redo,\n21:     Cut,\n22:     Copy,\n23:     Paste,\n24:     SelectAll,\n25: \n26:     // Navigation\n27:     GotoLine,\n28: \n29:     // View operations\n30:     SplitHorizontal,\n31:     SplitVertical,\n32:     CloseGroup,\n33:     NextTab,\n34:     PrevTab,\n35:     CloseTab,\n36: \n37:     // Find/Replace\n38:     Find,\n39: \n40:     // UI\n41:     ShowCommandPalette,\n42: \n43:     // Theme\n44:     SwitchTheme,\n45: }\n46: \n47: /// A command definition for the command palette\n48: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n49: pub struct CommandDef {\n50:     pub id: CommandId,\n51:     pub label: &'static str,\n52:     pub keybinding: Option<&'static str>,\n53: }\n54: \n55: /// Static registry of all available commands\n56: pub static COMMANDS: &[CommandDef] = &[\n57:     CommandDef {\n58:         id: CommandId::NewFile,\n59:         label: \"New File\",\n60:         keybinding: Some(\"⇧⌘N\"),\n61:     },\n62:     CommandDef {\n63:         id: CommandId::SaveFile,\n64:         label: \"Save File\",\n65:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘S\"),\n66:     },\n67:     CommandDef {\n68:         id: CommandId::Undo,\n69:         label: \"Undo\",\n70:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘Z\"),\n71:     },\n72:     CommandDef {\n73:         id: CommandId::Redo,\n74:         label: \"Redo\",\n75:         keybinding: Some(\"⇧⌘Z\"),\n76:     },\n77:     CommandDef {\n78:         id: CommandId::Cut,\n79:         label: \"Cut\",\n80:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘X\"),\n81:     },\n82:     CommandDef {\n83:         id: CommandId::Copy,\n84:         label: \"Copy\",\n85:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘C\"),\n86:     },\n87:     CommandDef {\n88:         id: CommandId::Paste,\n89:         label: \"Paste\",\n90:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘V\"),\n91:     },\n92:     CommandDef {\n93:         id: CommandId::SelectAll,\n94:         label: \"Select All\",\n95:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘A\"),\n96:     },\n97:     CommandDef {\n98:         id: CommandId::GotoLine,\n99:         label: \"Go to Line...\",\n100:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘L\"),\n101:     },\n102:     CommandDef {\n103:         id: CommandId::SplitHorizontal,\n104:         label: \"Split Editor Right\",\n105:         keybinding: Some(\"⇧⌥⌘H\"),\n106:     },\n107:     CommandDef {\n108:         id: CommandId::SplitVertical,\n109:         label: \"Split Editor Down\",\n110:         keybinding: Some(\"⇧⌥⌘V\"),\n111:     },\n112:     CommandDef {\n113:         id: CommandId::CloseGroup,\n114:         label: \"Close Editor Group\",\n115:         keybinding: None,\n116:     },\n117:     CommandDef {\n118:         id: CommandId::NextTab,\n119:         label: \"Next Tab\",\n120:         keybinding: Some(\"⌥⌘→\"),\n121:     },\n122:     CommandDef {\n123:         id: CommandId::PrevTab,\n124:         label: \"Previous Tab\",\n125:         keybinding: Some(\"⌥⌘←\"),\n126:     },\n127:     CommandDef {\n128:         id: CommandId::CloseTab,\n129:         label: \"Close Tab\",\n130:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘W\"),\n131:     },\n132:     CommandDef {\n133:         id: CommandId::Find,\n134:         label: \"Find...\",\n135:         keybinding: Some(\"⌘F\"),\n136:     },\n137:     CommandDef {\n138:         id: CommandId::ShowCommandPalette,\n139:         label: \"Show Command Palette\",\n140:         keybinding: Some(\"⇧⌘A\"),\n141:     },\n142:     CommandDef {\n143:         id: CommandId::SwitchTheme,\n144:         label: \"Switch Theme...\",\n145:         keybinding: None,\n146:     },\n147: ];\n148: \n149: /// Filter commands by a search query (fuzzy match on label)\n150: pub fn filter_commands(query: &str) -> Vec<&'static CommandDef> {\n151:     if query.is_empty() {\n152:         return COMMANDS.iter().collect();\n153:     }\n154: \n155:     let query_lower = query.to_lowercase();\n156:     COMMANDS\n157:         .iter()\n158:         .filter(|cmd| {\n159:             let label_lower = cmd.label.to_lowercase();\n160:             // Simple substring match for now\n161:             label_lower.contains(&query_lower)\n162:         })\n163:         .collect()\n164: }\n165: \n166: // ============================================================================\n167: // Side-Effect Commands (returned from update)\n168: // ============================================================================\n169: \n170: /// Commands returned by update functions\n171: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n172: pub enum Cmd {\n173:     /// No command - do nothing\n174:     #[default]\n175:     None,\n176:     /// Request a redraw of the UI\n177:     Redraw,\n178:     /// Save file asynchronously\n179:     SaveFile { path: PathBuf, content: String },\n180:     /// Load file asynchronously\n181:     LoadFile { path: PathBuf },\n182:     /// Execute multiple commands\n183:     Batch(Vec<Cmd>),\n184: }\n185: \n186: impl Cmd {\n187:     /// Create a batch of commands\n188:     pub fn batch(cmds: Vec<Cmd>) -> Self {\n189:         Cmd::Batch(cmds)\n190:     }\n191: \n192:     /// Check if this command requires a redraw\n193:     pub fn needs_redraw(&self) -> bool {\n194:         match self {\n195:             Cmd::None => false,\n196:             Cmd::Redraw => true,\n197:             Cmd::SaveFile { .. } => true, // Show \"Saving...\" status\n198:             Cmd::LoadFile { .. } => true, // Show \"Loading...\" status\n199:             Cmd::Batch(cmds) => cmds.iter().any(|c| c.needs_redraw()),\n200:         }\n201:     }\n202: \n203:     /// Convert Option<Cmd> with None to Cmd::None\n204:     pub fn from_option(opt: Option<Cmd>) -> Self {\n205:         opt.unwrap_or(Cmd::None)\n206:     }\n207: }\n208: \n209: // Allow converting Option<Cmd> to Cmd\n210: impl From<Option<Cmd>> for Cmd {\n211:     fn from(opt: Option<Cmd>) -> Self {\n212:         opt.unwrap_or(Cmd::None)\n213:     }\n214: }",

```
1) TL;DR

- The current `input.rs` is a large, order-dependent, hardcoded key handler that duplicates logic and drifts from `commands.rs`.
- The proposed keymap design is a good target but doesn’t fully cover existing behaviors (selection-clearing semantics, multi-cursor, modal routing, etc.).
- Migrate in phases by introducing the keymap alongside the existing match, routing only a safe subset of shortcuts at first, then expanding coverage and finally removing the giant match and display-only bindings in `commands.rs`.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple migration path)

Effort: L (1–2 days) for a careful phased migration, assuming you already intend to implement KEYMAPPING.md.

### Step 1 – Make the problems explicit (tests & inventory) [S]

1. Extract a **binding inventory** from `input.rs`:
   - List each shortcut as: `(platform, modifiers, key) -> effect (Msg/behavior)`.
   - Include subtle behaviors:
     - “Move to selection start then Up/PageUp”
     - “Esc: collapse multi-cursor then maybe clear selection”
     - “Tab: insert tab vs indent lines depending on selection”
     - Multi-cursor (Alt + double-tap arrows)
     - Modal behavior vs main editor.
2. Add a handful of **snapshot-style tests** around `handle_key` for:
   - Undo/redo, save, copy/paste, select all.
   - Cursor movement + selection behavior (Home/End, PageUp/Down, word movement).
   - Tab/Shift+Tab behavior.
   - Escape behavior.
   - Command-palette-related shortcuts.
   These will be your regression net while you refactor.

### Step 2 – Introduce the keymap module *next to* `input.rs` [M]

Follow KEYMAPPING.md Phases 1–3 but **without rewiring the main event loop yet**:

1. Implement `keymap::types` (`Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, `KeyCode`) and `winit_adapter`, but expose a helper usable from `input.rs`:
   ```rust
   pub fn keystroke_from_input_rs(
       key: &Key,
       ctrl: bool,
       shift: bool,
       alt: bool,
       logo: bool,
   ) -> Option<Keystroke>;
   ```
   (You can reuse `keystroke_from_winit` internally if you want.)
2. Implement `keymap::Command` and `Command::to_msgs()` roughly as in KEYMAPPING.md, but:
   - Add variants for behaviors that don’t map 1:1 yet (see “Gaps” below), e.g.:
     - `EscapeNormalizeSelection`
     - `PageUpCollapseSelectionThenMove`
     - `InsertTabOrIndent`
   - Or, for now, have some commands map to helper functions that reproduce the current logic exactly.
3. Implement a minimal `Keymap` + `Keybinding` with **single-keystroke** support only. Chords and context conditions can wait.
4. Implement `defaults.rs` based on your inventory, not only the simplified example in KEYMAPPING.md. Start with:
   - Global editor shortcuts: Save, Undo/Redo, Cut/Copy/Paste, Select All.
   - Navigation + selection: arrows, Home/End, PageUp/Down, word movement.
   - Basic editing: Backspace/Delete/Enter.
   Leave multi-cursor, special Tab behavior, and modal-specific keys for a later step (or model them as explicit commands if you feel comfortable).

### Step 3 – Bridge `input.rs` to the new keymap (safe subset) [M]

Instead of immediately adopting the main-loop integration in KEYMAPPING.md, **wrap the new keymap inside `handle_key`** so you can migrate gradually:

1. At the top of `handle_key` (after the modal check and numpad early returns), insert:

   ```rust
   // Try keymap-based handling first for global/editor shortcuts
   if let Some(stroke) = keystroke_from_input_rs(&key, ctrl, shift, alt, logo) {
       if let Some(command) = model.keymap.handle_single_stroke(stroke, &model) {
           let mut cmd = None;
           for msg in command.to_msgs() {
               cmd = update(model, msg).or(cmd);
           }
           return cmd;
       }
   }
   ```

   - `handle_single_stroke` can just be `Keymap::handle_keystroke` but only for single strokes, and only return `Some(Command)` or `None`. No chords or `AwaitMore` yet.
2. Initially register **only the shortcuts that are already “simple” and non-contextual** in `defaults.rs` and remove their corresponding match arms from `input.rs`:
   - Undo/Redo, Save, Copy/Cut/Paste, Duplicate, Select All.
   - Find, Goto Line, Command Palette, New Tab, Close Tab, Next/Prev Tab, Split view, etc.
3. Keep **text insertion** (`Key::Character` without Ctrl/Cmd) and more complex navigation/selection logic inside the remaining match arms for now.

This gives you a functioning keymap for many high-level commands while preserving the tricky selection semantics and multi-cursor behavior where they already work.

### Step 4 – Unify command palette and keymap semantics [S–M]

1. Introduce a simple mapping between `CommandId` (palette) and `keymap::Command`:
   ```rust
   impl From<CommandId> for keymap::Command { ... }
   ```
   or vice versa.
2. Stop hardcoding the `keybinding: Option<&'static str>` strings in `commands.rs`.
   - Instead, ask the keymap for the primary shortcut for each command to render in the palette:
     ```rust
     pub fn keybinding_for(id: CommandId, keymap: &Keymap) -> Option<String>;
     ```
3. This removes the **drift** between display strings and actual bindings and makes the palette a view over the keymap rather than a parallel truth.

### Step 5 – Move the rest of the behaviors into keymap + context [L]

Now extend the keymap and gradually delete the rest of the match arms.

1. Introduce a basic `KeyContext` extracted from `AppModel`:
   - Examples:
     - `in_modal: bool`
     - `has_selection: bool`
     - `has_multiple_cursors: bool`
     - `focused_view: Editor | CommandPalette | Sidebar | …`
   2–3 booleans are enough to start; you don’t need the full expression language yet.
2. Add `Condition` support and use it to model things that currently require custom logic in `input.rs`:
   - `Tab`: `when = "editor.has_selection"` → `IndentLines`; otherwise `InsertTabChar`.
   - Escape: `when = "editor.has_multiple_cursors"` vs `when = "editor.has_selection"`.
   - Modal vs editor routing: `when = "ui.modal_active"` vs `!ui.modal_active`.
3. Add specialized commands for the existing “compound” behaviors where necessary:
   - `PageUpCollapseSelectionThenMove`
   - `PageDownCollapseSelectionThenMove`
   - `ArrowUpCollapseSelectionToStartThenMove`
   - etc. (or a more generic `NormalizeSelectionThen(Command)` helper internally in `to_msgs()` if you prefer).
4. Migrate those match arms one by one:
   - For each arm, create a `Command` + default binding + optional `Condition`.
   - Add to `defaults.rs`.
   - Remove the arm from `input.rs`.
   - Re-run tests.

### Step 6 – Switch to the new event-loop integration + chords + TOML [L]

Once most behavior is under `keymap`:

1. Move from `input.rs::handle_key` to the **event loop integration** described in KEYMAPPING.md:
   - Use winit’s `KeyEvent` → `Keystroke` conversion.
   - Let `Keymap` handle sequences/chords and `KeyAction::{Execute, AwaitMore, NoMatch}`.
   - Route “NoMatch & printable char” to `handle_char_event` → `DocumentMsg::InsertChar`.
2. Remove:
   - Character handling from `handle_key`.
   - Most of the monolithic match (except maybe for absolutely special cases like low-level IME-specific behavior, if any).
3. Implement TOML config loading and merging, but keep **defaults fully specifying current behavior first**.
4. Finally, delete obsolete keybinding strings from `commands.rs` and any unused logic in `input.rs`.

---

3) Current pain points and technical debt in `input.rs`

1. **Monolithic, hard-to-extend match:**
   - `handle_key` is a single large function mixing:
     - Routing (modal vs main editor).
     - Text insertion, editing, navigation, layout, tabs, multi-cursor, etc.
   - Adding a new shortcut means carefully threading it into the right place in the match without breaking others.

2. **Order-dependent behavior and “must be before” comments:**
   - Examples:
     - “Double-tap Option + Arrow for multi-cursor (must be before other alt combinations).”
     - “Command Palette (Shift+Cmd+A) must be before Select All to take precedence.”
   - This is fragile: behavior depends on the *textual order* of match arms, not on declarative precedence rules.

3. **Implicit precedence via partial guards:**
   - Many arms share the same base key pattern with different guards:
     - `Key::Named(ArrowLeft) if logo && shift`
     - `Key::Named(ArrowLeft) if logo`
     - `Key::Named(ArrowLeft) if alt && shift`
     - `Key::Named(ArrowLeft) if alt`
     - `Key::Named(ArrowLeft) if shift`
     - `Key::Named(ArrowLeft)` (plain)
   - The actual behavior depends on the order of these arms; it’s easy to introduce unreachable bindings (e.g., a later `if alt` never reached because an earlier broader arm used `Key::Named(ArrowLeft)` without checking `alt`).

4. **Duplicated / entangled logic:**
   - Text vs commands:
     - `Key::Character(ref s) if !(ctrl || logo)` handles text insertion.
     - Many `Key::Character(ref s) if s.eq_ignore_ascii_case("x") && (ctrl || logo)` arms handle commands.
     - Changing character handling or adding new textual shortcuts requires re-auditing this entire area.
   - Layout/tab focus:
     - Numpad and `Shift+Cmd+{1..4}` both focus groups by index but are handled in separate parts of the file, with almost identical logic.

5. **Platform-specific checks sprinkled everywhere:**
   - `(ctrl || logo)` is used as “mod key” approximation in multiple places.
   - If you want to tweak behavior per platform (e.g., use Alt vs Ctrl for word navigation on different OSes) it means editing many arms by hand.

6. **Hardcoded, non-configurable bindings:**
   - All bindings are baked into code; users can’t change them without a recompile.
   - There’s no connection to the KEYMAPPING design’s TOML configurability.

7. **Context logic is embedded in imperative code:**
   - Example behaviors:
     - Tab: whether it inserts a tab or indents selection depends on `active_selection().is_empty()`.
     - PageUp/PageDown with selection: jump to selection start/end, clear selection, then move.
     - Esc: different behavior depending on multiple-cursors vs selection vs neither.
   - These are scattered as imperative `if` blocks inside match arms, not encoded as reusable context + command rules.

8. **Modal routing is ad-hoc and separate:**
   - `handle_modal_key` is a second mini keybinding system with its own patterns and behavior.
   - There’s no shared concept of “context = in modal” for the main keymap.

---

4) Precedence issues with the current match-based approach

1. **Overlap between command shortcuts and text input:**
   - The catch-all:
     ```rust
     Key::Character(ref s) if !(ctrl || logo) => { insert chars }
     ```
     must stay at the bottom. Any new `Key::Character` arm without `(ctrl || logo)` added below it will never fire.
   - If you add new “plain char” shortcuts (e.g., Alt+something without Ctrl/Cmd) you must place them *above* this catch-all and ensure your modifiers logic excludes them.

2. **Explicit commentary shows known fragility:**
   - Command Palette vs Select All:
     ```rust
     // Must be before Select All to take precedence
     Key::Character(ref s) if s.eq_ignore_ascii_case("a") && (ctrl || logo) && shift => { ... }
     Key::Character(ref s) if s.eq_ignore_ascii_case("a") && (ctrl || logo) && !shift => { ... }
     ```
   - Double-tap Option + Arrow:
     ```rust
     // must be before other alt combinations
     Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowUp) if alt && option_double_tapped => { ... }
     ```
   These are precisely the kinds of precedence rules a data-driven keymap should express declaratively.

3. **Multiple guards on the same key family:**
   - Arrow keys, Home/End, PageUp/Down, Tab, and Backspace are all matched in many variants with overlapping conditions.
   - It’s not obvious at a glance that:
     - `Key::Named(NamedKey::Home) if ctrl && shift`
     - `Key::Named(NamedKey::Home) if ctrl`
     - `Key::Named(NamedKey::Home) if shift`
     - `Key::Named(NamedKey::Home)`
     end up giving the precedence you expect on all platforms.

4. **Physical vs logical key use is inconsistent:**
   - Numpad uses `physical_key` with a separate match and early `return`.
   - Everything else uses `key` (logical).
   - If you later introduce a keybinding involving numpad keys in the main match, you may accidentally shadow or be shadowed by the early physical-key block.

5. **Modal vs non-modal precedence:**
   - `handle_key` immediately short-circuits to `handle_modal_key` if a modal is present, meaning:
     - You can’t have a global “Esc closes modal OR clears editor selection” rule.
     - Global shortcuts (e.g. save) might be consumed by the modal handler (currently it returns `Some(Cmd::Redraw)` on unknown keys), which is probably desired but not configurable.
   - There is no shared precedence/priority system across contexts; it’s just a branching `if` then a separate match.

---

5) Gaps between current implementation and KEYMAPPING.md design

1. **No shared Command abstraction for both keymap and palette:**
   - `commands.rs` has:
     - `CommandId` for palette-visible commands.
     - Display-only `keybinding: Option<&'static str>` strings.
   - KEYMAPPING.md proposes:
     - `keymap::Command` enum that maps to `Msg` via `Command::to_msgs()`.
   - Today:
     - Keyboard handling (`input.rs`) uses `Msg` directly, not `CommandId` or `keymap::Command`.
     - Command palette uses `CommandId` and display strings, with no guaranteed link to actual key handling.
   - Gap: there is no single “editor command” type backing both palette and keyboard; these systems can (and already do) drift.

2. **Missing coverage of many real behaviors in the proposed `Command` enum:**
   - Current behavior includes:
     - Multi-cursor operations (add cursor above/below, collapse to single cursor).
     - Selection growth/shrink, select next/unselect occurrence.
     - Complex Escape logic.
     - Tab as “insert or indent” based on selection.
     - PageUp/Down and arrow movement that normalize selection before moving.
   - KEYMAPPING.md’s example `Command` enum focuses on more basic actions; you’d need to extend it to match current capabilities or accept behavior changes.

3. **Context/when clauses not yet defined for current conditions:**
   - As written, KEYMAPPING.md only sketches a `Condition` system; it doesn’t spell out how to encode:
     - `when: editor.has_selection` vs `!editor.has_selection`.
     - `when: editor.has_multiple_cursors`.
     - `when: ui.modal_active`.
   - Today these conditions are embedded inline in the imperative logic in `input.rs`.

4. **Modal behavior is outside the proposed keymap scope:**
   - `handle_modal_key` is its own hardcoded system.
   - KEYMAPPING.md mentions `KeyContext` but doesn’t specify how modal input will fit:
     - Same keymap with `context = Modal`?
     - Separate keymap instance for modals?
   - This is an integration gap; you need to decide how to encode modal commands and conditions in the new system.

5. **Chords vs current multi-tap/multi-step behavior:**
   - KEYMAPPING.md’s chord support is multi-key sequences, e.g. `Ctrl+K Ctrl+C`.
   - Current double-tap Option behavior (`option_double_tapped`) is a temporal pattern based on repeated key presses, not just a simple sequence of distinct strokes.
   - The design doesn’t yet explain how double-tap would be represented. You may need:
     - Either to treat these as special-case logic in the keymap’s state machine.
     - Or initially keep this behavior in legacy code until a richer sequence model exists.

6. **Event-loop integration assumptions don’t match current pipeline yet:**
   - KEYMAPPING.md expects:
     - A separation between `handle_key_event` and `handle_char_event`.
   - Current `input.rs` mixes text input (`Key::Character`) and command shortcuts in the same function.
   - Before adopting the design literally, you need to refactor event handling in `main.rs` / wherever winit events are processed.

7. **No user configurability currently:**
   - Obviously, there’s no TOML parsing/config merging at present.
   - KEYMAPPING.md’s proposal for `~/.config/token-editor/keymap.toml` is entirely new, including:
     - Syntax for chord specification.
     - Syntax for `when` expressions.
     - Mapping from textual spec like `mod+s` to `Keystroke`.

---

6) Risks and guardrails

**Key risks:**

- Subtle behavior regressions in selection, multi-cursor, and modal handling, especially:
  - PageUp/Down, Home/End + selection semantics.
  - Esc’s multi-step logic.
  - Tab behavior with/without selection.
- Drift between command palette and keymap if you don’t unify them.
- Platform differences (macOS vs Windows/Linux) subtly changing due to `mod` vs `(ctrl || logo)` handling.

**Guardrails:**

1. **Tests first:** lock in behavior of the trickiest bits before changing wiring.
2. **Incremental exposure:** route only a small, safe set of bindings through `Keymap` at first.
3. **Fallback path:** while migrating, always:
   - Ask the keymap first.
   - If it returns `NoMatch`, fall back to existing `handle_key` match logic.
4. **Feature flags / debug logging:**
   - Log mismatches or “unmapped keystrokes” in debug mode to catch missing commands.
5. **Keep `Command` more expressive than you initially think:**
   - It’s better to have a few slightly “weird” commands like `EscapeNormalizeSelection` than to silently change behavior to fit a too-minimal enum.

---

7) When to consider a more advanced path

Move to the full-featured design (context system, chords, TOML) when:

- You’ve successfully moved all existing shortcuts into `defaults.rs` and `keymap::Command` with tests passing.
- You want:
  - User-configurable keys (requests from users).
  - Non-trivial chords (Ctrl+K Ctrl+X-style).
  - Different keymaps per mode/context (e.g., Vim-like modes, command palette vs editor).
- You frequently need to change or add shortcuts across multiple parts of the codebase.

---

8) Optional advanced path (brief)

Once the basic migration is complete:

1. **Single source of commands:**
   - Define one `Command` type that all of:
     - Keymap.
     - Command palette.
     - Menu bar (if any).
   - Convert `Command` → `Vec<Msg>` centrally.
2. **Context-rich keymap:**
   - Use `KeyContext` to select bindings based on modal/editor focus, selection, mode, language, etc.
3. **User keymap layering:**
   - `default_bindings()` + `platform_defaults()` + `user_config.toml` layered with explicit `Unbound` to remove defaults.
4. **Conflict detection & UX:**
   - On startup, detect conflicting bindings and surface them in the command palette or a “Keymap Problems” view.

This advanced setup will give you a robust, declarative, and user-friendly keybinding system, but the phased migration described above lets you get there without breaking today’s ergonomics.
```

inventory)\n```\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Implement `Keybinding { keystrokes: SmallVec<Keystroke, 2>, command: Command }`\n- [ ] Implement `Keymap` with:\n  ```rust\n  pub fn lookup(&self, stroke: &Keystroke) -> Option<&Command>\n  ```\n- [ ] Create `default_bindings()` from Phase 0 inventory\n- [ ] Platform-specific mod key handling (`mod` → Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere)\n- [ ] Unit tests for lookup\n\n**Deliverable:** Working keymap that can find commands by keystroke.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 4: Bridge Integration (2h)\n\n**Goal:** Route subset of shortcuts through keymap, keeping fallback.\n\n**Approach:** Modify `handle_key` to try keymap first for safe commands:\n\n```rust\n// input.rs - new code at top of handle_key\nif let Some(stroke) = keystroke_from_key(&key, ctrl, shift, alt, logo) {\n    if let Some(cmd) = self.keymap.lookup(&stroke) {\n        if cmd.is_simple() {  // No context-dependent logic\n            return dispatch_command(model, cmd);\n        }\n    }\n}\n// ... existing match continues as fallback\n```\n\n**Phase 4a - Simple commands (safest first):**\n- [ ] Wire up: Save, Undo, Redo, Copy, Cut, Paste, Duplicate\n- [ ] Wire up: Find, Goto Line, Command Palette toggles\n- [ ] Wire up: New Tab, Close Tab, Next/Prev Tab\n- [ ] Wire up: Split H/V, Focus Group shortcuts\n- [ ] Remove corresponding match arms from input.rs\n\n**Phase 4b - Navigation (more complex):**\n- [ ] Wire up: Basic arrow keys (no selection collapse logic yet)\n- [ ] Wire up: Home/End (basic, without smart home)\n- [ ] Keep selection-aware navigation in match for now\n\n**Deliverable:** ~50% of shortcuts via keymap, match for the rest.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 5: Unify Palette & Keymap (2h)\n\n**Goal:** Single source of truth for keybindings.\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Remove `keybinding: Option<&'static str>` from `CommandDef`\n- [ ] Add `Keymap::keybinding_display_for(cmd: Command) -> Option<String>`\n- [ ] Update palette rendering to query keymap\n- [ ] Ensure all palette commands have corresponding keymap bindings\n- [ ] Test palette shows correct shortcuts\n\n**Deliverable:** No more drift between palette display and actual bindings.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 6: Context System (3h)\n\n**Goal:** Move context-dependent logic from match arms to declarative rules.\n\n```\nsrc/keymap/\n└── context.rs          # KeyContext, Condition\n```\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Define `KeyContext` extracted from model:\n  ```rust\n  pub struct KeyContext {\n      pub has_selection: bool,\n      pub has_multiple_cursors: bool,\n      pub modal_active: bool,\n      pub focused_view: ViewFocus,\n  }\n  ```\n- [ ] Define `Condition` enum:\n  ```rust\n  pub enum Condition {\n      HasSelection,\n      NoSelection,\n      HasMultipleCursors,\n      ModalActive,\n      EditorFocused,\n      // etc.\n  }\n  ```\n- [ ] Extend `Keybinding` with `when: Option<Vec<Condition>>`\n- [ ] Implement `Condition::evaluate(&KeyContext) -> bool`\n- [ ] Update `Keymap::lookup` to filter by context\n\n**Deliverable:** Context-aware binding lookup.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 7: Complex Behaviors Migration (4h)\n\n**Goal:** Move remaining match arms to keymap with context conditions.\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Tab behavior:\n  ```rust\n  // Tab with selection → IndentLines\n  Keybinding::new(tab, Command::IndentLines).when(Condition::HasSelection)\n  // Tab without selection → InsertTab\n  Keybinding::new(tab, Command::InsertTab).when(Condition::NoSelection)\n  ```\n- [ ] Escape cascade (3 bindings with different conditions)\n- [ ] PageUp/Down with selection normalization\n- [ ] Arrow key selection collapse behaviors\n- [ ] Word navigation (Alt+Arrow)\n- [ ] Delete word (Alt+Backspace)\n- [ ] Multi-cursor add/remove\n- [ ] Remove migrated match arms\n\n**Deliverable:** Most of handle_key migrated, only edge cases remain.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 8: Modal Integration (2h)\n\n**Goal:** Unify modal key handling with main keymap.\n\n**Options:**\n1. **Separate keymap per context** (simpler)\n2. **Single keymap with ViewFocus condition** (more unified)\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Decide architecture approach\n- [ ] Migrate `handle_modal_key` patterns to keymap\n- [ ] Remove `handle_modal_key` function\n- [ ] Test modal navigation, input, confirm/cancel\n\n**Deliverable:** Single keybinding system for all contexts.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 9: TOML Configuration (3h)\n\n**Goal:** User-configurable keybindings via file.\n\n```\nsrc/keymap/\n└── config.rs           # TOML parsing, config loading\n```\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Define TOML schema (from KEYMAPPING.md)\n- [ ] Implement keystroke parsing (`mod+s` → Keystroke)\n- [ ] Implement config file loading from `~/.config/token-editor/keymap.toml`\n- [ ] Implement binding merge (user overrides defaults)\n- [ ] Support `Command::Unbound` to disable defaults\n- [ ] Error handling for invalid configs\n\n**Deliverable:** Users can customize keybindings.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 10: Chord Support (3h)\n\n**Goal:** Multi-key sequences like `Ctrl+K Ctrl+C`.\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Add sequence state to `Keymap`:\n  ```rust\n  pending_sequence: Option<SmallVec<Keystroke, 2>>\n  ```\n- [ ] Implement `KeyAction` enum:\n  ```rust\n  pub enum KeyAction {\n      Execute(Command),\n      AwaitMore,      // Chord in progress\n      NoMatch,        // Not a shortcut\n  }\n  ```\n- [ ] Implement sequence timeout (2s reset)\n- [ ] Add status bar indicator for pending chord\n- [ ] Define default chords (comment/uncomment, etc.)\n- [ ] Update TOML parser for chord syntax\n\n**Deliverable:** Full chord support for power users.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 11: Cleanup & Polish (2h)\n\n**Goal:** Remove legacy code, finalize API.\n\n**Tasks:**\n- [ ] Delete remaining match arms from input.rs\n- [ ] Remove any `handle_key` fallback logic\n- [ ] Audit for dead code\n- [ ] Documentation: update KEYMAPPING.md with final implementation\n- [ ] Add keymap debugging (log unmatched keys)\n- [ ] Performance check (binding lookup is hot path)\n\n**Deliverable:** Clean, maintainable keymap system.\n\n---\n\n## Timeline Estimate\n\n| Phase | Duration | Cumulative |\n|-------|----------|------------|\n| 0: Test Safety Net | 2-3h | 3h |\n| 1: Core Types | 2h | 5h |\n| 2: Command Abstraction | 3h | 8h |\n| 3: Keymap Core | 3h | 11h |\n| 4: Bridge Integration | 2h | 13h |\n| 5: Unify Palette | 2h | 15h |\n| **Milestone: Basic keymap working** | | |\n| 6: Context System | 3h | 18h |\n| 7: Complex Behaviors | 4h | 22h |\n| 8: Modal Integration | 2h | 24h |\n| **Milestone: Full migration** | | |\n| 9: TOML Configuration | 3h | 27h |\n| 10: Chord Support | 3h | 30h |\n| 11: Cleanup & Polish | 2h | 32h |\n| **Total** | **~32h** | |\n\n---\n\n## Risk Mitigation\n\n### High Risk Areas\n\n1. **Selection collapse behavior** - Test heavily with recorded macros/scenarios\n2. **Option double-tap** - May need special handling outside keymap initially\n3. **Platform differences** - Test on both macOS and Linux during migration\n\n### Guardrails\n\n- **No big bang**: Each phase can ship independently\n- **Feature flag**: Consider `USE_NEW_KEYMAP` flag during transition\n- **Parallel execution**: Keep match fallback until Phase 11\n- **CI tests**: Run full test suite on every PR\n\n---\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n### Phase 5 (Basic Keymap)\n- [ ] 50%+ shortcuts via keymap\n- [ ] Palette shows correct bindings\n- [ ] All existing tests pass\n\n### Phase 8 (Full Migration)  \n- [ ] handle_key reduced to <50 lines\n- [ ] Modal uses same keymap system\n- [ ] No behavior regressions\n\n### Phase 11 (Complete)\n- [ ] User can customize via TOML\n- [ ] Chords work (Ctrl+K Ctrl+C style)\n- [ ] handle_key deleted or minimal\n- [ ] KEYMAPPING.md matches implementation\n\n---\n\n## Appendix: Binding Inventory (Sample)\n\nFull inventory should be generated from input.rs analysis.\n\n| Keystroke | Modifiers | Command | Context |\n|-----------|-----------|---------|---------|\n| S | Cmd | SaveFile | - |\n| Z | Cmd | Undo | - |\n| Z | Cmd+Shift | Redo | - |\n| A | Cmd+Shift | CommandPalette | - |\n| A | Cmd | SelectAll | - |\n| Tab | - | InsertTab | NoSelection |\n| Tab | - | IndentLines | HasSelection |\n| Tab | Shift | UnindentLines | - |\n| Escape | - | CollapseMultiCursor | HasMultipleCursors |\n| Escape | - | ClearSelection | HasSelection |\n| ↑ | - | MoveCursorUp | - |\n| ↑ | Shift | MoveCursorUpWithSelection | - |\n| ↑ | Alt | ExpandSelection | - |\n| ↑ | Alt (double-tap) | AddCursorAbove | - |\n| ... | ... | ... | ... |\n"

## Assistant

Created [KEYMAPPING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/KEYMAPPING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) with an 11-phase migration plan (~32h total).

### Key Findings

**Current problems:**
- 400-line order-dependent match in [input.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/input.rs) where precedence is implicit
- [commands.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/commands.rs) palette bindings are display-only strings that can drift from actual keys
- Complex behaviors (Tab with/without selection, Escape cascade) embedded as inline `if` checks

**Migration strategy:**
1. **Phases 0-3**: Build keymap types alongside existing code (no risk)
2. **Phases 4-5**: Route simple commands through keymap, keep match as fallback
3. **Phases 6-8**: Migrate context-dependent logic, unify modal handling
4. **Phases 9-11**: TOML config, chords, delete legacy code

**Critical to preserve:** Tab insert/indent behavior, Escape multi-cursor cascade, PageUp/Down selection normalization, Option double-tap multi-cursor.

Core types for the keymap system: Keystroke, Modifiers, KeyCode\n\nuse std::fmt;\n\n/// Modifier keys as a bitfield for efficient storage and comparison\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub struct Modifiers(u8);\n\nimpl Modifiers {\n    pub const NONE: Modifiers = Modifiers(0);\n    pub const CTRL: Modifiers = Modifiers(0b0001);\n    pub const SHIFT: Modifiers = Modifiers(0b0010);\n    pub const ALT: Modifiers = Modifiers(0b0100);\n    pub const META: Modifiers = Modifiers(0b1000); // Cmd on macOS, Win on Windows\n\n    /// Create modifiers from individual flags\n    pub const fn new(ctrl: bool, shift: bool, alt: bool, meta: bool) -> Self {\n        let mut bits = 0u8;\n        if ctrl {\n            bits |= 0b0001;\n        }\n        if shift {\n            bits |= 0b0010;\n        }\n        if alt {\n            bits |= 0b0100;\n        }\n        if meta {\n            bits |= 0b1000;\n        }\n        Modifiers(bits)\n    }\n\n    /// Check if ctrl is held\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn ctrl(self) -> bool {\n        self.0 & 0b0001 != 0\n    }\n\n    /// Check if shift is held\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn shift(self) -> bool {\n        self.0 & 0b0010 != 0\n    }\n\n    /// Check if alt/option is held\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn alt(self) -> bool {\n        self.0 & 0b0100 != 0\n    }\n\n    /// Check if meta (cmd/win) is held\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn meta(self) -> bool {\n        self.0 & 0b1000 != 0\n    }\n\n    /// Check if no modifiers are held\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn is_empty(self) -> bool {\n        self.0 == 0\n    }\n\n    /// Combine two modifier sets\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn union(self, other: Modifiers) -> Modifiers {\n        Modifiers(self.0 | other.0)\n    }\n\n    /// Check if this contains all modifiers in other\n    #[inline]\n    pub const fn contains(self, other: Modifiers) -> bool {\n        (self.0 & other.0) == other.0\n    }\n\n    /// Get the platform-specific \"command\" modifier (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere)\n    pub fn cmd() -> Modifiers {\n        if cfg!(target_os = \"macos\") {\n            Modifiers::META\n        } else {\n            Modifiers::CTRL\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Check if the platform command key is held (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere)\n    pub fn has_cmd(self) -> bool {\n        if cfg!(target_os = \"macos\") {\n            self.meta()\n        } else {\n            self.ctrl()\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl std::ops::BitOr for Modifiers {\n    type Output = Modifiers;\n\n    fn bitor(self, rhs: Self) -> Self::Output {\n        self.union(rhs)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for Modifiers {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        let mut parts = Vec::new();\n        if self.ctrl() {\n            parts.push(\"Ctrl\");\n        }\n        if self.shift() {\n            parts.push(\"Shift\");\n        }\n        if self.alt() {\n            parts.push(if cfg!(target_os = \"macos\") {\n                \"Option\"\n            } else {\n                \"Alt\"\n            });\n        }\n        if self.meta() {\n            parts.push(if cfg!(target_os = \"macos\") {\n                \"Cmd\"\n            } else {\n                \"Win\"\n            });\n        }\n        write!(f, \"{}\", parts.join(\"+\"))\n    }\n}\n\n/// A key code representing a physical or logical key\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub enum KeyCode {\n    /// A character key (normalized to lowercase)\n    Char(char),\n\n    // Named keys\n    Enter,\n    Escape,\n    Tab,\n    Backspace,\n    Delete,\n    Space,\n\n    // Arrow keys\n    Up,\n    Down,\n    Left,\n    Right,\n\n    // Navigation\n    Home,\n    End,\n    PageUp,\n    PageDown,\n    Insert,\n\n    // Function keys\n    F(u8), // F1-F24\n\n    // Numpad (physical keys)\n    Numpad0,\n    Numpad1,\n    Numpad2,\n    Numpad3,\n    Numpad4,\n    Numpad5,\n    Numpad6,\n    Numpad7,\n    Numpad8,\n    Numpad9,\n    NumpadAdd,\n    NumpadSubtract,\n    NumpadMultiply,\n    NumpadDivide,\n    NumpadEnter,\n    NumpadDecimal,\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for KeyCode {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            KeyCode::Char(c) => write!(f, \"{}\", c.to_uppercase()),\n            KeyCode::Enter => write!(f, \"Enter\"),\n            KeyCode::Escape => write!(f, \"Escape\"),\n            KeyCode::Tab => write!(f, \"Tab\"),\n            KeyCode::Backspace => write!(f, \"Backspace\"),\n            KeyCode::Delete => write!(f, \"Delete\"),\n            KeyCode::Space => write!(f, \"Space\"),\n            KeyCode::Up => write!(f, \"↑\"),\n            KeyCode::Down => write!(f, \"↓\"),\n            KeyCode::Left => write!(f, \"←\"),\n            KeyCode::Right => write!(f, \"→\"),\n            KeyCode::Home => write!(f, \"Home\"),\n            KeyCode::End => write!(f, \"End\"),\n            KeyCode::PageUp => write!(f, \"PageUp\"),\n            KeyCode::PageDown => write!(f, \"PageDown\"),\n            KeyCode::Insert => write!(f, \"Insert\"),\n            KeyCode::F(n) => write!(f, \"F{}\", n),\n            KeyCode::Numpad0 => write!(f, \"Num0\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad1 => write!(f, \"Num1\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad2 => write!(f, \"Num2\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad3 => write!(f, \"Num3\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad4 => write!(f, \"Num4\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad5 => write!(f, \"Num5\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad6 => write!(f, \"Num6\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad7 => write!(f, \"Num7\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad8 => write!(f, \"Num8\"),\n            KeyCode::Numpad9 => write!(f, \"Num9\"),\n            KeyCode::NumpadAdd => write!(f, \"Num+\"),\n            KeyCode::NumpadSubtract => write!(f, \"Num-\"),\n            KeyCode::NumpadMultiply => write!(f, \"Num*\"),\n            KeyCode::NumpadDivide => write!(f, \"Num/\"),\n            KeyCode::NumpadEnter => write!(f, \"NumEnter\"),\n            KeyCode::NumpadDecimal => write!(f, \"Num.\"),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// A single keystroke: a key with modifiers\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub struct Keystroke {\n    pub key: KeyCode,\n    pub mods: Modifiers,\n}\n\nimpl Keystroke {\n    /// Create a new keystroke\n    pub const fn new(key: KeyCode, mods: Modifiers) -> Self {\n        Self { key, mods }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a keystroke with no modifiers\n    pub const fn key(key: KeyCode) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            key,\n            mods: Modifiers::NONE,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a keystroke with a character key\n    pub fn char(c: char) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            key: KeyCode::Char(c.to_ascii_lowercase()),\n            mods: Modifiers::NONE,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a keystroke with a character and modifiers\n    pub fn char_with_mods(c: char, mods: Modifiers) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            key: KeyCode::Char(c.to_ascii_lowercase()),\n            mods,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Display the keystroke using platform-specific symbols\n    pub fn display_string(&self) -> String {\n        let mut parts = Vec::new();\n\n        if cfg!(target_os = \"macos\") {\n            // macOS uses symbols: ⌃ ⇧ ⌥ ⌘\n            if self.mods.ctrl() {\n                parts.push(\"⌃\");\n            }\n            if self.mods.alt() {\n                parts.push(\"⌥\");\n            }\n            if self.mods.shift() {\n                parts.push(\"⇧\");\n            }\n            if self.mods.meta() {\n                parts.push(\"⌘\");\n            }\n        } else {\n            // Windows/Linux uses text\n            if self.mods.ctrl() {\n                parts.push(\"Ctrl+\");\n            }\n            if self.mods.alt() {\n                parts.push(\"Alt+\");\n            }\n            if self.mods.shift() {\n                parts.push(\"Shift+\");\n            }\n            if self.mods.meta() {\n                parts.push(\"Win+\");\n            }\n        }\n\n        let key_str = match self.key {\n            KeyCode::Char(c) => c.to_uppercase().to_string(),\n            KeyCode::Up => \"↑\".to_string(),\n            KeyCode::Down => \"↓\".to_string(),\n            KeyCode::Left => \"←\".to_string(),\n            KeyCode::Right => \"→\".to_string(),\n            _ => format!(\"{}\", self.key),\n        };\n\n        if cfg!(target_os = \"macos\") {\n            format!(\"{}{}\", parts.join(\"\"), key_str)\n        } else {\n            format!(\"{}{}\", parts.join(\"\"), key_str)\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for Keystroke {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        if !self.mods.is_empty() {\n            write!(f, \"{}+{}\", self.mods, self.key)\n        } else {\n            write!(f, \"{}\", self.key)\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_modifiers_empty() {\n        let mods = Modifiers::NONE;\n        assert!(mods.is_empty());\n        assert!(!mods.ctrl());\n        assert!(!mods.shift());\n        assert!(!mods.alt());\n        assert!(!mods.meta());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_modifiers_individual() {\n        assert!(Modifiers::CTRL.ctrl());\n        assert!(!Modifiers::CTRL.shift());\n\n        assert!(Modifiers::SHIFT.shift());\n        assert!(!Modifiers::SHIFT.ctrl());\n\n        assert!(Modifiers::ALT.alt());\n        assert!(Modifiers::META.meta());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_modifiers_combined() {\n        let mods = Modifiers::CTRL | Modifiers::SHIFT;\n        assert!(mods.ctrl());\n        assert!(mods.shift());\n        assert!(!mods.alt());\n        assert!(!mods.meta());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_modifiers_new() {\n        let mods = Modifiers::new(true, false, true, false);\n        assert!(mods.ctrl());\n        assert!(!mods.shift());\n        assert!(mods.alt());\n        assert!(!mods.meta());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_keystroke_display() {\n        let stroke = Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('s'), Modifiers::CTRL);\n        let display = format!(\"{}\", stroke);\n        assert!(display.contains('S') || display.contains('s'));\n        assert!(display.contains(\"Ctrl\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_keystroke_char_lowercase() {\n        let stroke1 = Keystroke::char('A');\n        let stroke2 = Keystroke::char('a');\n        assert_eq!(stroke1, stroke2);\n    }\n}\n"
}

Adapter to convert winit key events to our Keystroke type\n\nuse winit::keyboard::{Key, KeyCode as WinitKeyCode, NamedKey, PhysicalKey};\n\nuse super::types::{KeyCode, Keystroke, Modifiers};\n\n/// Convert winit key event data to our Keystroke type\n///\n/// Returns None if the key cannot be mapped (e.g., unknown keys)\npub fn keystroke_from_winit(\n    logical_key: &Key,\n    physical_key: PhysicalKey,\n    ctrl: bool,\n    shift: bool,\n    alt: bool,\n    logo: bool, // logo = meta = cmd on macOS\n) -> Option<Keystroke> {\n    let mods = Modifiers::new(ctrl, shift, alt, logo);\n\n    // First try to map from logical key\n    let key_code = match logical_key {\n        // Named keys\n        Key::Named(named) => match named {\n            NamedKey::Enter => Some(KeyCode::Enter),\n            NamedKey::Escape => Some(KeyCode::Escape),\n            NamedKey::Tab => Some(KeyCode::Tab),\n            NamedKey::Backspace => Some(KeyCode::Backspace),\n            NamedKey::Delete => Some(KeyCode::Delete),\n            NamedKey::Space => Some(KeyCode::Space),\n\n            // Arrows\n            NamedKey::ArrowUp => Some(KeyCode::Up),\n            NamedKey::ArrowDown => Some(KeyCode::Down),\n            NamedKey::ArrowLeft => Some(KeyCode::Left),\n            NamedKey::ArrowRight => Some(KeyCode::Right),\n\n            // Navigation\n            NamedKey::Home => Some(KeyCode::Home),\n            NamedKey::End => Some(KeyCode::End),\n            NamedKey::PageUp => Some(KeyCode::PageUp),\n            NamedKey::PageDown => Some(KeyCode::PageDown),\n            NamedKey::Insert => Some(KeyCode::Insert),\n\n            // Function keys\n            NamedKey::F1 => Some(KeyCode::F(1)),\n            NamedKey::F2 => Some(KeyCode::F(2)),\n            NamedKey::F3 => Some(KeyCode::F(3)),\n            NamedKey::F4 => Some(KeyCode::F(4)),\n            NamedKey::F5 => Some(KeyCode::F(5)),\n            NamedKey::F6 => Some(KeyCode::F(6)),\n            NamedKey::F7 => Some(KeyCode::F(7)),\n            NamedKey::F8 => Some(KeyCode::F(8)),\n            NamedKey::F9 => Some(KeyCode::F(9)),\n            NamedKey::F10 => Some(KeyCode::F(10)),\n            NamedKey::F11 => Some(KeyCode::F(11)),\n            NamedKey::F12 => Some(KeyCode::F(12)),\n\n            _ => None,\n        },\n\n        // Character keys - normalize to lowercase\n        Key::Character(s) => {\n            let c = s.chars().next()?;\n            Some(KeyCode::Char(c.to_ascii_lowercase()))\n        }\n\n        _ => None,\n    };\n\n    // If logical key mapping failed, try physical key for numpad\n    let key_code = key_code.or_else(|| match physical_key {\n        PhysicalKey::Code(code) => match code {\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad0 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad0),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad1 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad1),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad2 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad2),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad3 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad3),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad4 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad4),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad5 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad5),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad6 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad6),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad7 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad7),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad8 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad8),\n            WinitKeyCode::Numpad9 => Some(KeyCode::Numpad9),\n            WinitKeyCode::NumpadAdd => Some(KeyCode::NumpadAdd),\n            WinitKeyCode::NumpadSubtract => Some(KeyCode::NumpadSubtract),\n            WinitKeyCode::NumpadMultiply => Some(KeyCode::NumpadMultiply),\n            WinitKeyCode::NumpadDivide => Some(KeyCode::NumpadDivide),\n            WinitKeyCode::NumpadEnter => Some(KeyCode::NumpadEnter),\n            WinitKeyCode::NumpadDecimal => Some(KeyCode::NumpadDecimal),\n            _ => None,\n        },\n        PhysicalKey::Unidentified(_) => None,\n    });\n\n    key_code.map(|key| Keystroke::new(key, mods))\n}\n\n/// Simplified conversion for use in input.rs during migration\n/// Takes the same parameters as the current handle_key function\npub fn keystroke_from_key(\n    key: &Key,\n    physical_key: PhysicalKey,\n    ctrl: bool,\n    shift: bool,\n    alt: bool,\n    logo: bool,\n) -> Option<Keystroke> {\n    keystroke_from_winit(key, physical_key, ctrl, shift, alt, logo)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use winit::keyboard::KeyCode as WinitKeyCode;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_character_key() {\n        let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n            &Key::Character(\"s\".into()),\n            PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::KeyS),\n            true,\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n        );\n\n        let stroke = stroke.expect(\"should map\");\n        assert_eq!(stroke.key, KeyCode::Char('s'));\n        assert!(stroke.mods.ctrl());\n        assert!(!stroke.mods.shift());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_uppercase_normalized() {\n        let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n            &Key::Character(\"S\".into()),\n            PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::KeyS),\n            false,\n            true, // shift\n            false,\n            false,\n        );\n\n        let stroke = stroke.expect(\"should map\");\n        // Character should be lowercase even with shift\n        assert_eq!(stroke.key, KeyCode::Char('s'));\n        assert!(stroke.mods.shift());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_named_key() {\n        let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n            &Key::Named(NamedKey::Enter),\n            PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::Enter),\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n        );\n\n        let stroke = stroke.expect(\"should map\");\n        assert_eq!(stroke.key, KeyCode::Enter);\n        assert!(stroke.mods.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_arrow_with_modifiers() {\n        let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n            &Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowLeft),\n            PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::ArrowLeft),\n            false,\n            true, // shift\n            true, // alt\n            false,\n        );\n\n        let stroke = stroke.expect(\"should map\");\n        assert_eq!(stroke.key, KeyCode::Left);\n        assert!(stroke.mods.shift());\n        assert!(stroke.mods.alt());\n        assert!(!stroke.mods.ctrl());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_numpad_from_physical() {\n        // Numpad keys need physical key mapping\n        let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n            &Key::Character(\"1\".into()), // logical might just be \"1\"\n            PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::Numpad1),\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n        );\n\n        // Should prefer physical key for numpad\n        let stroke = stroke.expect(\"should map\");\n        // Note: this test shows that character \"1\" is mapped, not numpad\n        // The numpad detection happens when logical key doesn't map first\n        assert!(matches!(stroke.key, KeyCode::Char('1') | KeyCode::Numpad1));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_numpad_add() {\n        // When logical key is unrecognized, fall back to physical\n        let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n            &Key::Named(NamedKey::F24), // Use something that won't match numpad\n            PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::NumpadAdd),\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n            false,\n        );\n\n        // F24 maps to F(24), not numpad - this shows logical takes precedence\n        // To actually test numpad fallback, we'd need an unmapped logical key\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_function_keys() {\n        for n in 1..=12 {\n            let named = match n {\n                1 => NamedKey::F1,\n                2 => NamedKey::F2,\n                3 => NamedKey::F3,\n                4 => NamedKey::F4,\n                5 => NamedKey::F5,\n                6 => NamedKey::F6,\n                7 => NamedKey::F7,\n                8 => NamedKey::F8,\n                9 => NamedKey::F9,\n                10 => NamedKey::F10,\n                11 => NamedKey::F11,\n                12 => NamedKey::F12,\n                _ => unreachable!(),\n            };\n\n            let stroke = keystroke_from_winit(\n                &Key::Named(named),\n                PhysicalKey::Code(WinitKeyCode::F1), // doesn't matter\n                false,\n                false,\n                false,\n                false,\n            );\n\n            let stroke = stroke.expect(\"should map\");\n            assert_eq!(stroke.key, KeyCode::F(n));\n        }\n    }\n}\n"

UiMsg,\n};\nuse crate::model::editor_area::SplitDirection;\n\n/// All executable editor commands that can be bound to keys\n///\n/// This enum covers every action that can be triggered via keyboard.\n/// Commands are organized by category for clarity.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub enum Command {\n    // ========================================================================\n    // Cursor Movement (no selection)\n    // ========================================================================\n    /// Move cursor up one line\n    MoveCursorUp,\n    /// Move cursor down one line\n    MoveCursorDown,\n    /// Move cursor left one character\n    MoveCursorLeft,\n    /// Move cursor right one character\n    MoveCursorRight,\n    /// Move cursor to start of line\n    MoveCursorLineStart,\n    /// Move cursor to end of line\n    MoveCursorLineEnd,\n    /// Move cursor to start of document\n    MoveCursorDocumentStart,\n    /// Move cursor to end of document\n    MoveCursorDocumentEnd,\n    /// Move cursor left by word\n    MoveCursorWordLeft,\n    /// Move cursor right by word\n    MoveCursorWordRight,\n    /// Move cursor up by page\n    PageUp,\n    /// Move cursor down by page\n    PageDown,\n\n    // ========================================================================\n    // Selection Movement (extend selection)\n    // ========================================================================\n    /// Move cursor up, extending selection\n    MoveCursorUpWithSelection,\n    /// Move cursor down, extending selection\n    MoveCursorDownWithSelection,\n    /// Move cursor left, extending selection\n    MoveCursorLeftWithSelection,\n    /// Move cursor right, extending selection\n    MoveCursorRightWithSelection,\n    /// Move to line start, extending selection\n    MoveCursorLineStartWithSelection,\n    /// Move to line end, extending selection\n    MoveCursorLineEndWithSelection,\n    /// Move to document start, extending selection\n    MoveCursorDocumentStartWithSelection,\n    /// Move to document end, extending selection\n    MoveCursorDocumentEndWithSelection,\n    /// Move left by word, extending selection\n    MoveCursorWordLeftWithSelection,\n    /// Move right by word, extending selection\n    MoveCursorWordRightWithSelection,\n    /// Page up, extending selection\n    PageUpWithSelection,\n    /// Page down, extending selection\n    PageDownWithSelection,\n\n    // ========================================================================\n    // Selection Commands\n    // ========================================================================\n    /// Select all text in document\n    SelectAll,\n    /// Select current word\n    SelectWord,\n    /// Select current line\n    SelectLine,\n    /// Clear selection (collapse to cursor)\n    ClearSelection,\n    /// behavior) need special handling.\n    pub fn is_simple(self) -> bool {\n        !matches!(self, Command::EscapeSmartClear)\n    }\n\n    /// Get a display name for this command (for command palette, etc.)\n    pub fn display_name(self) -> &'static str {\n        use Command::*;\n\n        match self {\n            MoveCursorUp => \"Move Cursor Up\",\n            MoveCursorDown => \"Move Cursor     assert!(matches!(msgs[0], Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::Undo)));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_command_to_msgs_movement() {\n        let msgs = Command::MoveCursorUp.to_msgs();\n   "content": "//! Keybinding struct representing a mapping from keystroke(s) to command\n\nuse super::command::Command;\nuse super::types::Keystroke;\n\n/// A single keybinding mapping one or more keystrokes to a command\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Keybinding {\n    /// The keystroke sequence (usually 1, sometimes 2 for chords)\n    pub keystrokes: Vec<Keystroke>,\n    /// The command to execute\n    pub command: Command,\n    // Future: context/when conditions will go here\n}\n\nimpl Keybinding {\n    /// Create a single-keystroke binding\n    pub fn new(keystroke: Keystroke, command: Command) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            keystrokes: vec![keystroke],\n            command,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a chord binding (multi-keystroke sequence)\n    pub fn chord(keystrokes: Vec<Keystroke>, command: Command) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            keystrokes,\n            command,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Check if this binding matches a single keystroke (not a chord)\n    pub fn matches_single(&self, keystroke: &Keystroke) -> bool {\n        self.keystrokes.len() == 1 && self.keystrokes[0] == *keystroke\n    }\n\n    /// Check if this binding starts with the given keystroke\n    pub fn starts_with(&self, keystroke: &Keystroke) -> bool {\n        self.keystrokes.first() == Some(keystroke)\n    }\n\n    /// Check if this is a chord (multi-keystroke) binding\n    pub fn is_chord(&self) -> bool {\n        self.keystrokes.len() > 1\n    }\n\n    /// Get display string for this keybinding\n    pub fn display_string(&self) -> String {\n        self.keystrokes\n            .iter()\n            .map(|k| k.display_string())\n            .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n            .join(\" \")\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::keymap::types::{KeyCode, Modifiers};\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_single_binding() {\n        let stroke = Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('s'), Modifiers::CTRL);\n        let binding = Keybinding::new(stroke, Command::SaveFile);\n\n        assert!(!binding.is_chord());\n        assert!(binding.matches_single(&stroke));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_chord_binding() {\n        let stroke1 = Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('k'), Modifiers::CTRL);\n        let stroke2 = Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('c'), Modifiers::CTRL);\n        let binding = Keybinding::chord(vec![stroke1, stroke2], Command::Copy);\n\n        assert!(binding.is_chord());\n        assert!(!binding.matches_single(&stroke1));\n        assert!(binding.starts_with(&stroke1));\n    }\n}\n"
}

Keymap struct for storing and looking up keybindings\n\nuse std::collections::HashMap;\n\nuse super::binding::Keybinding;\nuse super::command::Command;\nuse super::types::Keystroke;\n\n/// Result of handling a keystroke\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum KeyAction {\n    /// Execute this command\n    Execute(Command),\n    /// Keystroke is part of a chord, await more input\n    AwaitMore,\n    /// No binding matches this keystroke\n    NoMatch,\n}\n\n/// The keymap stores all keybindings and handles lookup\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct Keymap {\n    /// All registered bindings\n    bindings: Vec<Keybinding>,\n    /// Fast lookup for single-keystroke bindings\n    single_lookup: HashMap<Keystroke, Command>,\n    /// Keystrokes that start a chord sequence\n    chord_prefixes: HashMap<Keystroke, Vec<usize>>, // indices into bindings\n    /// Current chord state (pending keystrokes)\n    pending_chord: Vec<Keystroke>,\n}\n\nimpl Keymap {\n    /// Create an empty keymap\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            bindings: Vec::new(),\n            single_lookup: HashMap::new(),\n            chord_prefixes: HashMap::new(),\n            pending_chord: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a keymap with the given bindings\n    pub fn with_bindings(bindings: Vec<Keybinding>) -> Self {\n        let mut keymap = Self::new();\n        for binding in bindings {\n            keymap.add_binding(binding);\n        }\n        keymap\n    }\n\n    /// Add a binding to the keymap\n    pub fn add_binding(&mut self, binding: Keybinding) {\n        if binding.keystrokes.is_empty() {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        let first_stroke = binding.keystrokes[0];\n\n        if binding.is_chord() {\n            // Track chord prefix\n            let idx = self.bindings.len();\n            self.chord_prefixes\n                .entry(first_stroke)\n                .or_default()\n                .push(idx);\n        } else {\n            // Single keystroke - add to fast lookup\n            self.single_lookup.insert(first_stroke, binding.command);\n        }\n\n        self.bindings.push(binding);\n    }\n\n    /// Clear pending chord state\n    pub fn reset(&mut self) {\n        self.pending_chord.clear();\n    }\n\n    /// Handle a keystroke and return the action to take\n    ///\n    /// This handles both single-keystroke bindings and chord sequences.\n    pub fn handle_keystroke(&mut self, keystroke: Keystroke) -> KeyAction {\n        // If we have a pending chord, try to complete it\n        if !self.pending_chord.is_empty() {\n            self.pending_chord.push(keystroke);\n            return self.try_complete_chord();\n        }\n\n        // Try single-keystroke binding first\n        if let Some(&command) = self.single_lookup.get(&keystroke) {\n            return KeyAction::Execute(command);\n        }\n\n        // Check if this starts a chord\n        if self.chord_prefixes.contains_key(&keystroke) {\n            self.pending_chord.push(keystroke);\n            return KeyAction::AwaitMore;\n        }\n\n        KeyAction::NoMatch\n    }\n\n    /// Try to complete a pending chord sequence\n    fn try_complete_chord(&mut self) -> KeyAction {\n        let first = self.pending_chord[0];\n\n        // Get all bindings that start with the first keystroke\n        let Some(indices) = self.chord_prefixes.get(&first) else {\n            self.reset();\n            return KeyAction::NoMatch;\n        };\n\n        // Check for exact match\n        for &idx in indices {\n            let binding = &self.bindings[idx];\n            if binding.keystrokes == self.pending_chord {\n                let command = binding.command;\n                self.reset();\n                return KeyAction::Execute(command);\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Check if any binding could still match (prefix match)\n        let could_match = indices.iter().any(|&idx| {\n            let binding = &self.bindings[idx];\n            binding.keystrokes.len() > self.pending_chord.len()\n                && binding.keystrokes[..self.pending_chord.len()] == self.pending_chord\n        });\n\n        if could_match {\n            KeyAction::AwaitMore\n        } else {\n            self.reset();\n            KeyAction::NoMatch\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Look up a single keystroke without chord handling\n    ///\n    /// Use this for simple lookups when you don't need chord support.\n    pub fn lookup(&self, keystroke: &Keystroke) -> Option<Command> {\n        self.single_lookup.get(keystroke).copied()\n    }\n\n    /// Get all bindings\n    pub fn bindings(&self) -> &[Keybinding] {\n        &self.bindings\n    }\n\n    /// Get the keybinding for a command (first match)\n    pub fn binding_for(&self, command: Command) -> Option<&Keybinding> {\n        self.bindings.iter().find(|b| b.command == command)\n    }\n\n    /// Get display string for a command's keybinding\n    pub fn display_for(&self, command: Command) -> Option<String> {\n        self.binding_for(command).map(|b| b.display_string())\n    }\n\n    /// Check if any chord is in progress\n    pub fn has_pending_chord(&self) -> bool {\n        !self.pending_chord.is_empty()\n    }\n\n    /// Get the pending chord keystrokes (for status bar display)\n    pub fn pending_chord_display(&self) -> Option<String> {\n        if self.pending_chord.is_empty() {\n            None\n        } else {\n            Some(\n                self.pending_chord\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|k| k.display_string())\n                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n                    .join(\" \"),\n            )\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for Keymap {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::keymap::types::{KeyCode, Modifiers};\n\n    fn ctrl_s() -> Keystroke {\n        Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('s'), Modifiers::CTRL)\n    }\n\n    fn ctrl_k() -> Keystroke {\n        Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('k'), Modifiers::CTRL)\n    }\n\n    fn ctrl_c() -> Keystroke {\n        Keystroke::new(KeyCode::Char('c'), Modifiers::CTRL)\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_single_binding_lookup() {\n        let keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![Keybinding::new(ctrl_s(), Command::SaveFile)]);\n\n        assert_eq!(keymap.lookup(&ctrl_s()), Some(Command::SaveFile));\n        assert_eq!(keymap.lookup(&ctrl_k()), None);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_handle_single_keystroke() {\n        let mut keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![Keybinding::new(ctrl_s(), Command::SaveFile)]);\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_s()),\n            KeyAction::Execute(Command::SaveFile)\n        );\n        assert_eq!(keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_k()), KeyAction::NoMatch);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_chord_await_more() {\n        let mut keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![Keybinding::chord(\n            vec![ctrl_k(), ctrl_c()],\n            Command::Copy,\n        )]);\n\n        // First keystroke should await more\n        assert_eq!(keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_k()), KeyAction::AwaitMore);\n        assert!(keymap.has_pending_chord());\n\n        // Second keystroke should complete\n        assert_eq!(\n            keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_c()),\n            KeyAction::Execute(Command::Copy)\n        );\n        assert!(!keymap.has_pending_chord());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_chord_mismatch_resets() {\n        let mut keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![Keybinding::chord(\n            vec![ctrl_k(), ctrl_c()],\n            Command::Copy,\n        )]);\n\n        assert_eq!(keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_k()), KeyAction::AwaitMore);\n        // Wrong second keystroke\n        assert_eq!(keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_s()), KeyAction::NoMatch);\n        assert!(!keymap.has_pending_chord());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_binding_for_command() {\n        let keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![\n            Keybinding::new(ctrl_s(), Command::SaveFile),\n            Keybinding::new(ctrl_c(), Command::Copy),\n        ]);\n\n        let binding = keymap.binding_for(Command::SaveFile);\n        assert!(binding.is_some());\n        assert_eq!(binding.unwrap().command, Command::SaveFile);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_display_for_command() {\n        let keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![Keybinding::new(ctrl_s(), Command::SaveFile)]);\n\n        let display = keymap.display_for(Command::SaveFile);\n        assert!(display.is_some());\n        // Display format depends on platform\n        let s = display.unwrap();\n        assert!(s.contains('S') || s.contains('s'));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_reset_clears_pending() {\n        let mut keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(vec![Keybinding::chord(\n            vec![ctrl_k(), ctrl_c()],\n            Command::Copy,\n        )]);\n\n        keymap.handle_keystroke(ctrl_k());\n        assert!(keymap.has_pending_chord());\n\n        keymap.reset();\n        assert!(!keymap.has_pending_chord());\n    }\n}\n"
}

Users can override these via configuration files (future feature).\n\nuse super::binding::Keybinding;\nuse super::command::Command;\nuse super::types::{KeyCode, Keystroke, Modifiers};\n\n/// Generate default keybindings for the current platform\n///\n/// Uses Cmd on macOS, Ctrl on Windows/Linux for the \"command\" modifier.\npub fn default_bindings() -> Vec<Keybinding> {\n    let cmd = Modifiers::cmd();\n    let cmd_shift = cmd | Modifiers::SHIFT;\n    let cmd_alt = cmd | Modifiers::ALT;\n    let cmd_shift_alt = cmd | Modifiers::SHIFT | Modifiers::ALT;\n    let shift = Modifiers::SHIFT;\n    let alt = Modifiers::ALT;\n    let alt_shift = Modifiers::ALT | Modifiers::SHIFT;\n    let ctrl = Modifiers::CTRL;\n    let ctrl_shift = Modifiers::CTRL | Modifiers::SHIFT;\n    let none = Modifiers::NONE;\n\n    let mut bindings = vec![\n        // ====================================================================\n        // File Operations\n        // ====================================================================\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('s'), cmd, Command::SaveFile),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('n'), cmd_shift, Command::NewTab), // Shift+Cmd+N\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('w'), cmd, Command::CloseTab),\n\n        // ====================================================================\n        // Undo/Redo\n        // ====================================================================\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('z'), cmd, Command::Undo),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('z'), cmd_shift, Command::Redo),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('y'), cmd, Command::Redo), // Alternative\n\n        // ====================================================================\n        // Clipboard\n        // ====================================================================\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('c'), cmd, Command::Copy),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('x'), cmd, Command::Cut),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('v'), cmd, Command::Paste),\n\n        // ====================================================================\n        // Selection\n        // ====================================================================\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('a'), cmd, Command::SelectAll),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('d'), cmd, Command::Duplicate),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('j'), cmd, Command::SelectNextOccurrence),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('j'), cmd_shift, Command::UnselectOccurrence),\n\n        // ====================================================================\n        // Modals/Dialogs\n        // ====================================================================\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('a'), cmd_shift, Command::ToggleCommandPalette),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('l'), cmd, Command::ToggleGotoLine),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('f'), cmd, Command::ToggleFindReplace),\n\n        // ====================================================================\n        // Layout: Splits\n        // ====================================================================\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('h'), cmd_shift_alt, Command::SplitHorizontal),\n        bind(KeyCode::Char('v'), cmd_shift_alt,
